  "content": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{Models and Connectors}\n\\label{sec:models}\n\nWe evaluate token pruning across six VLA architectures spanning three vision connector families (Table~\\ref{tab:model_properties}; Figure~\\ref{fig:architecture}). These connectors differ in whether they process ViT patches independently or create cross-token dependencies.\n\n\\textbf{Linear projection} applies a single matrix multiplication per patch. $\\pi_{0}$~\\cite{black2024pi0} projects 256 patches through a linear layer; $\\pi_{0.5}$~\\cite{black2025pi05} uses a $\\times\\!\\sqrt{d}$-scaled linear projection across 768 tokens from three cameras (CV$\\,{<}\\,$0.06).\n\n\\textbf{MLP projector} maps each patch independently through a multi-layer perceptron. OpenVLA~\\cite{kim2024openvla} uses a three-layer MLP (256 tokens; CV$\\,{=}\\,$0.215); OFT~\\cite{kim2025openoft} shares the same connector (512 tokens; CV$\\,{=}\\,$0.219).\n\n\\textbf{Token-entangling connectors} introduce cross-token dependencies. X-VLA~\\cite{zheng2025xvla} feeds tokens through a 12-layer BART encoder~\\cite{lewis2020bart} (50 tokens). SmolVLA~\\cite{shukor2025smolvla} applies pixel-shuffle $4{\\times}4$ folding, concatenating 16 contiguous patches per output token (128 tokens).\n\nWe classify coupling as \\emph{independent} when each output token depends on a single input patch, and \\emph{entangling} when output tokens encode information from multiple patches. Four models are evaluated in closed-loop control on LIBERO~\\cite{liu2023libero}; $\\pi_{0}$ and OFT contribute to the CV diagnostic only.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/paper/fig_architecture_v2.pdf}\n\\caption{Vision connector architectures across four closed-loop VLAs, ordered by increasing compressibility fragility. Token-independent connectors ($\\pi_{0.5}$: linear; OpenVLA: MLP) yield low CV. Token-entangling connectors (X-VLA: BART encoder; SmolVLA: pixel-shuffle) create cross-token dependencies and higher CV, predicting threshold-graded or catastrophic pruning failure.}\n\\label{fig:architecture}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{VLA models evaluated. Coupling indicates independent vs.\\ cross-token processing. $N$ = vision tokens input to the LLM.}\n\\label{tab:model_properties}\n\\begin{tabular}{lrlcrll}\n\\toprule\nModel & Params & Connector & $N$ & CV & Coupling & Eval \\\\\n\\midrule\n$\\pi_{0.5}$ & 4B & Linear ($\\times\\!\\sqrt{d}$) & 768 & 0.055 & Indep.\\ & Closed-loop \\\\\nX-VLA & 880M & Linear + BART & 50 & 0.151 & Entangling & Closed-loop \\\\\nOpenVLA & 7B & 3-layer MLP & 256 & 0.215 & Indep.\\ & Closed-loop \\\\\nSmolVLA & 450M & Pixel-shuffle $4{\\times}4$ & 128 & 0.314 & Entangling & Closed-loop \\\\\n\\midrule\n$\\pi_{0}$ & 3.3B & Linear & 256 & 0.038 & Indep.\\ & Offline \\\\\nOFT$^\\dagger$ & 7B & 3-layer MLP & 512 & 0.219 & Indep.\\ & Offline \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\footnotesize $^\\dagger$ OpenVLA fine-tuning variant, included for CV calibration (offline metrics only).}\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Pruning Protocol}\n\\label{sec:pruning_protocol}\n\nWe apply post-connector token pruning at retention ratio $k \\in \\{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.85, 0.9, 0.95\\}$, retaining $\\lfloor k \\cdot N \\rfloor$ tokens. Two strategies bracket expected performance: \\textbf{L2 norm selection} retains the top-$k$ fraction by L2 norm, and \\textbf{random subsampling} selects $\\lfloor k \\cdot N \\rfloor$ tokens uniformly at random. The \\emph{importance margin} at ratio $k$ is $\\text{SR}_{\\text{L2}}(k) - \\text{SR}_{\\text{Random}}(k)$; a positive margin indicates token importance structure is informative. Both strategies physically remove unselected tokens and re-index position IDs. We prune post-connector to isolate the connector's effect on token information structure.\n\n\\subsection{Compressibility Diagnostic: Importance CV}\n\\label{sec:cv_diagnostic}\n\nWe quantify post-connector token heterogeneity via the coefficient of variation (CV) of L2 norms:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\text{CV} = \\frac{\\sigma\\!\\left(\\{\\|t_i\\|_2\\}_{i=1}^{N}\\right)}{\\mu\\!\\left(\\{\\|t_i\\|_2\\}_{i=1}^{N}\\right)}\n\\label{eq:cv}\n\\end{equation}\nCV is a single-pass diagnostic requiring one forward pass with no downstream evaluation. It captures a continuous spectrum from $\\pi_{0}$ (CV$\\,{=}\\,$0.038, near-uniform norms) to SmolVLA (CV$\\,{=}\\,$0.314, high heterogeneity). Low-CV models have near-uniform tokens where \\emph{which} tokens are removed matters more than \\emph{how many}; high-CV entangling connectors fail catastrophically under any removal strategy.\n\n\\subsection{Vision Token Re-encoder (VTR)}\n\\label{sec:vtr}\n\nHard pruning fails for token-entangling connectors because removing any token destroys spatially distributed information. VTR addresses this through learned re-encoding: a cross-attention module (${\\sim}$22M parameters, ${\\sim}$5\\% of SmolVLA) uses $M$ learned queries to attend over all $N$ source tokens. The compression ratio is controlled by $M \\in \\{16, 32, 64, 96, 128\\}$; $M{=}N$ serves as a passthrough control.\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-2/projects/importance_margin_vla_compress/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
  "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/paper/fig_hero_overview.pdf}\n\\caption{Overview of VLA token compressibility.\n\\textbf{(a)}~The same robot observation produces qualitatively different token importance patterns through different vision connectors, quantified by the coefficient of variation (CV) of post-connector token norms.\n\\textbf{(b)}~Connector architecture determines pruning tolerance: independent connectors degrade gracefully, entangling connectors exhibit cliffs; task complexity amplifies the divergence.\n\\textbf{(c)}~A single forward pass CV diagnostic routes each architecture to an appropriate compression strategy.}\n\\label{fig:teaser}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nRemove 5\\% of vision tokens from SmolVLA and its success rate collapses from 59.2\\% to near-zero (${\\leq}$6.6\\%). Apply the same reduction to $\\pi_{0.5}$ and it retains 98.2\\% under L2 norm selection. Both are Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models built on a ViT encoder feeding tokens through a connector into a language model backbone; the critical difference is the connector architecture. Token pruning is well-established for ViTs~\\cite{rao2021dynamicvit,bolya2023tome,liang2022evit} and extends to VLAs~\\cite{cheng2026vlaiap,xu2025vlacache,yang2025efficientvla,liu2025vlapruner}, but each method validates on a single architecture, implicitly assuming pruning transfers across designs.\n\nYet current VLAs employ fundamentally different vision connectors---linear projections~\\cite{black2024pi0}, MLP projectors~\\cite{kim2024openvla}, pixel-shuffle folding~\\cite{shukor2025smolvla}, and learned encoders with cross-token self-attention~\\cite{zheng2025xvla}---each imposing distinct constraints on how visual information distributes across tokens. Deploying a pruning method validated on one connector type to another risks silent degradation or complete failure.\n\nWe present an empirical study of VLA token compressibility, identifying the vision-language connector as the primary determinant of pruning robustness (Figure~\\ref{fig:teaser}). Token-independent connectors degrade gracefully; entangling connectors produce catastrophic failures amplified by task complexity ($20{\\times}$ from Object to Goal). The coefficient of variation (CV) of post-connector token L2 norms captures this on a continuous spectrum (0.038 to 0.314). Under sufficient compression, scoring effectiveness becomes connector-dependent: L2 norm aids $\\pi_{0.5}$ (up to $+$46.9\\,pp on Long at $k{=}0.9$; $+$35.2\\,pp on Goal at $k{=}0.85$) but reverses at aggressive ratios ($-$36\\,pp at $k{=}0.5$); on X-VLA it is anti-informative ($-$8 to $-$27\\,pp), while attention recovers near-baseline performance. For pixel-shuffle connectors where all pruning fails, Vision Token Re-encoder (VTR, ${\\sim}$5\\% extra parameters) recovers over 60\\% SR at 75\\% compression.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep]\n    \\item A compressibility analysis across five VLA architectures (450M--7B) on four LIBERO suites, identifying connector architecture as the primary determinant of pruning robustness.\n    \\item CV of token L2 norms as a single-pass diagnostic for connector compressibility type (uniform, graded, catastrophic), validated on four closed-loop architectures.\n    \\item A zero-mask ablation proving position disruption---not information loss---drives $\\pi_{0.5}$'s 89\\,pp failure gap. VTR recovers a pixel-shuffle VLA from 0\\% to over 60\\% at 75\\% compression.\n\\end{itemize}\n"
}

    "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\textwidth]{figures/paper/fig_hero_overview.pdf}\n\\caption{Overview of VLA token compressibility.\n\\textbf{(a)}~The same robot observation produces qualitatively different token importance patterns through different vision connectors, quantified by the coefficient of variation (CV) of post-connector token norms.\n\\textbf{(b)}~Connector architecture determines pruning tolerance: independent connectors degrade gracefully, entangling connectors exhibit cliffs; task complexity amplifies the divergence.\n\\textbf{(c)}~A single forward pass CV diagnostic routes each architecture to an appropriate compression strategy.}\n\\label{fig:teaser}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nRemove 5\\% of vision tokens from SmolVLA and its success rate collapses from 59.2\\% to near-zero (${\\leq}$6.6\\%). Apply the same reduction to $\\pi_{0.5}$ and it retains 98.2\\% under L2 norm selection. Both are Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models built on a ViT encoder feeding tokens through a connector into a language model backbone; the critical difference is the connector architecture. Token pruning is well-established for ViTs~\\cite{rao2021dynamicvit,bolya2023tome,liang2022evit} and extends to VLAs~\\cite{cheng2026vlaiap,xu2025vlacache,yang2025efficientvla,liu2025vlapruner}, but each method validates on a single architecture, implicitly assuming pruning transfers across designs.\n\nYet current VLAs employ fundamentally different vision connectors---linear projections~\\cite{black2024pi0}, MLP projectors~\\cite{kim2024openvla}, pixel-shuffle folding~\\cite{shukor2025smolvla}, and learned encoders with cross-token self-attention~\\cite{zheng2025xvla}---each imposing distinct constraints on how visual information distributes across tokens. Deploying a pruning method validated on one connector type to another risks silent degradation or complete failure.\n\nWe present an empirical study of VLA token compressibility, identifying the vision-language connector as the primary determinant of pruning robustness (Figure~\\ref{fig:teaser}). Token-independent connectors degrade gracefully; entangling connectors produce catastrophic failures amplified by task complexity ($20{\\times}$ from Object to Goal). The coefficient of variation (CV) of post-connector token L2 norms captures this on a continuous spectrum (0.038 to 0.314). Under sufficient compression, scoring effectiveness becomes connector-dependent: L2 norm aids $\\pi_{0.5}$ (up to $+$46.9\\,pp on Long at $k{=}0.9$; $+$35.2\\,pp on Goal at $k{=}0.85$) but reverses at aggressive ratios ($-$36\\,pp at $k{=}0.5$); on X-VLA it is anti-informative ($-$8 to $-$27\\,pp), while attention recovers near-baseline performance. For pixel-shuffle connectors where all pruning fails, Vision Token Re-encoder (VTR, ${\\sim}$5\\% extra parameters) recovers over 60\\% SR at 75\\% compression.\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{itemize}[nosep]\n    \\item A compressibility analysis across five VLA architectures (450M--7B) on four LIBERO suites, identifying connector architecture as the primary determinant of pruning robustness.\n    \\item CV of token L2 norms as a single-pass diagnostic for connector compressibility type (uniform, graded, catastrophic), validated on four closed-loop architectures.\n    \\item A zero-mask ablation proving position disruption---not information loss---drives $\\pi_{0.5}$'s 89\\,pp failure gap. VTR recovers a pixel-shuffle VLA from 0\\% to over 60\\% at 75\\% compression.\n\\end{itemize}\n"
  }
}

  "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related_work}\n\n\\paragraph{ViT token pruning.}\nDynamicViT~\\cite{rao2021dynamicvit}, EViT~\\cite{liang2022evit}, A-ViT~\\cite{yin2022avit}, and Token Merging~\\cite{bolya2023tome} remove 50--66\\% of tokens with under 0.5\\,pp ImageNet accuracy loss, but robotic manipulation inverts the spatial-insensitivity assumption. None has been evaluated in closed-loop VLA control.\n\n\\paragraph{Token efficiency in VLMs and VLAs.}\nFastV~\\cite{chen2024fastv} and PruMerge~\\cite{shang2024prumerge} reduce vision tokens in VLMs using attention signals. VLA-specific methods have emerged: VLA-IAP~\\cite{cheng2026vlaiap}, VLA-Cache~\\cite{xu2025vlacache}, EfficientVLA~\\cite{yang2025efficientvla}, VLA-Pruner~\\cite{liu2025vlapruner}, LightVLA~\\cite{jiang2026lightvla}, and Compressor-VLA~\\cite{gao2025compressorvla}. Each validates on a single architecture; we ask how architectural choices determine which compression approaches succeed or fail.\n\n\\paragraph{Vision connectors.}\nLLaVA~\\cite{liu2023llava} projects patches independently through an MLP; BLIP-2~\\cite{li2023blip2} uses cross-attention queries; pixel shuffle~\\cite{shi2016subpixel} folds spatial neighborhoods into channels. These create qualitatively different token dependencies whose pruning consequences have not been characterized. We provide the first cross-architecture analysis of how connector coupling and task complexity jointly govern VLA token compressibility.\n"
}

  "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nConnector architecture is the primary factor governing VLA token compressibility. Across five architectures and four LIBERO suites, token-independent connectors degrade gracefully; entangling connectors exhibit catastrophic cliffs amplified by task complexity ($20{\\times}$ from Object to Goal). CV partitions models into uniform, graded, and catastrophic classes in a single forward pass. Under sufficient compression, scoring effectiveness becomes connector- and k-dependent: at $k{=}0.9$ on $\\pi_{0.5}$ Long, L2 preserves 80\\% while random collapses to 34\\% (46.9\\,pp gap). A zero-mask ablation confirms position disruption---not information loss---drives the failure. Where all deletion fails, VTR recovers over 60\\% SR at 75\\% compression. Real-robot validation and adaptive per-task pruning schedules remain open. \\textbf{AI disclosure:} This work used AI tools (Claude) for code development and manuscript drafting; all scientific claims are the authors' own.\n"
}

_source line 12102 · 2026-05-28T13:53:05.189302+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	CV predicts connector-level compressibility, but task complexity determines \emph{which} scoring method succeeds within a given connector. On OpenVLA, L2 leads on single-step Object ($+$7.8\,pp at $k{=}0.9$) but the hierarchy inverts on multi-step Long, where Random outperforms both L2 and attention at $k{=}0.7$ (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}). The monotonic L2 curve versus the non-monotonic random curve on $\pi_{0.5}$ Long (Figure~\ref{fig:cliff_highlight}) reflects position encoding sensitivity: L2 preserves spatial locality after re-indexing, while random creates scattered gaps that maximally disrupt RoPE at $k{\approx}0.9$ before recovering at $k{\leq}0.7$ as surviving positions become approximately uniform (Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
5	
6	\paragraph{Limitations.}
7	All evaluations use LIBERO simulation; we provide no sim-to-real evidence. The six architectures differ in backbone, encoder, and scale beyond the connector, preventing causal isolation. The CV--SR correlation relies on four closed-loop models, too few for a general law. VTR is validated on SmolVLA only. Most conditions use a single seed; $\pi_{0.5}$ Long uses five-seed means ($n{=}5$, sample s.d.\ ${\leq}$2.7\,pp; Appendix~\ref{app:pi05_long_multiseed}); cross-seed validation on other models shows $\Delta \leq 5.8$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:pi05_multiseed},~\ref{app:openvla_multiseed}). Extended discussion in Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}.
8

### Tool result

_source line 12132 · 2026-05-28T13:55:56.291716+00:00_

1	\begin{abstract}
2	Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models encode observations as hundreds of vision tokens, creating a computational bottleneck for real-time robotic control. Token pruning offers a training-free remedy, yet its effect varies dramatically across architectures: at retention ratio $k{=}0.95$, $\pi_{0.5}$ preserves 98\% task success while SmolVLA collapses to 0\%---why?
3	
4	We identify the vision-language connector as the primary architectural factor governing pruning tolerance. The coefficient of variation (CV) of post-connector token norms, computable in a single forward pass, predicts compressibility across the full architecture spectrum: from CV$\,{=}\,$0.038 (near-immune linear projection) to CV$\,{=}\,$0.314 (catastrophic pixel-shuffle collapse). Across five VLA architectures (450\,M--7\,B parameters) and four LIBERO manipulation suites, token-independent connectors degrade gracefully, while token-entangling connectors exhibit abrupt cliffs: SmolVLA collapses when just 6 of 128 tokens are removed.
5	
6	Importance-based selection is not universally beneficial---it can be actively harmful. At moderate compression, L2 norm selection preserves 98\% success on $\pi_{0.5}$ where random yields 5\% (93\,pp gap); at aggressive ratios, random outperforms L2 by up to 36\,pp. Attention-based scoring exhibits within-model reversals across task suites. No scoring method is universally safe: task complexity amplifies connector-dependent patterns, with multi-step tasks magnifying entangling-connector losses by up to $20{\times}$. A zero-mask ablation isolates the mechanism: on $\pi_{0.5}$, zeroing 10\% of tokens preserves 98.0\% SR while physical removal collapses to 9.0\%---the 89\,pp gap is entirely attributable to position disruption, not information loss. For architectures where all deletion strategies fail, a lightweight re-encoding module (${\sim}$5\% extra parameters) recovers $>$60\% success at 75\% compression.
7	\end{abstract}
8

  "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nCV predicts connector-level compressibility, but task complexity determines \\emph{which} scoring method succeeds within a given connector---L2 leads on $\\pi_{0.5}$ Object but inverts on Long; on X-VLA, attention recovers near-baseline while L2 is anti-informative (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_discussion}). The V-shaped random recovery on $\\pi_{0.5}$ Long reflects position encoding sensitivity: scattered gaps maximally disrupt RoPE at $k{\\approx}0.9$, recovering as surviving positions become approximately uniform.\n\nConnector architecture is the primary factor governing VLA token compressibility. Token-independent connectors degrade gracefully; entangling connectors exhibit catastrophic cliffs amplified by task complexity ($20{\\times}$ from Object to Goal). CV partitions models in a single forward pass. Under sufficient compression, scoring effectiveness becomes connector- and $k$-dependent. Where all deletion fails, VTR recovers over 60\\% SR at 75\\% compression. Real-robot validation and adaptive per-task pruning schedules remain open.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations.}\nAll evaluations use LIBERO simulation; no sim-to-real evidence is provided. The six architectures differ in backbone, encoder, and scale beyond the connector. The CV--SR correlation relies on four closed-loop models, too few for a general law. VTR is validated on SmolVLA only. Most conditions use a single seed; $\\pi_{0.5}$ Long uses five-seed means ($n{=}5$, s.d.\\ ${\\leq}$2.7\\,pp). \\textbf{AI disclosure:} This work used AI tools (Claude) for code development and manuscript drafting; all scientific claims are the authors' own.\n"
}

_source line 12142 · 2026-05-28T13:56:33.357596+00:00_

&lt;tool_use_error>String to replace not found in file.
String: \textbf{Architecture-dependent scoring.} The optimal scoring method depends on the connector (Table~\ref{tab:attention_pruning}). On X-VLA at $k{=}0.5$, attention-based scoring recovers near-baseline performance (${\geq}$92.8\%) while L2 falls $8$--$27$\,pp below random---because the BART encoder's cross-token attention renders L2 norms anti-informative. On $\pi_{0.5}$, attention is catastrophically harmful on Object ($-$30.4\,pp) because attention scores select semantically relevant tokens that lack the spatial precision this architecture depends on. On Long, attention degrades steeply to a plateau ($-$26.5\,pp from $k{=}0.95$ to $k{=}0.85$, then flat through $k{=}0.5$; Figure~\ref{fig:cliff_highlight}).

\textbf{Disruption peak.} Random pruning on $\pi_{0.5}$ produces a counterintuitive failure: intermediate removal is \emph{more} destructive than aggressive removal. On Long at $k{=}0.9$, the margin peaks at 46.9\,pp (L2 80.4\% vs.\ Random 33.6\%; Figure~\ref{fig:cliff_highlight}). The margin follows an asymmetric inverted-U: $k{=}0.95$ (25.6\,pp) to crossing zero near $k{\approx}0.71$; at $k{=}0.5$, all methods converge within ${\sim}$2\,pp (Appendix Figure~\ref{fig:margin_profile}). On Object at $k{=}0.85$, random collapses to 3.6--5.6\% while L2 maintains 99.0\% ($+$90\,pp margin). The V-shaped random recovery arises from position aliasing under scattered removal (Section~\ref{sec:analysis}).

\textbf{Importance margin gradient.} L2's advantage shrinks monotonically with task complexity on $\pi_{0.5}$ at $k{=}0.85$: Object $+$93\,pp $>$ Goal $+$35.2\,pp $>$ Long $+$33.2\,pp. Multi-step tasks distribute critical information across tokens with heterogeneous norm profiles, making L2 norms a progressively weaker proxy for token utility.&lt;/tool_use_error>

85	\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/paper/fig_method_gap.pdf}
86	\caption{Method gap $|\text{SR(L2)} - \text{SR(Random)}|$ at $k{=}0.95$. $\pi_{0.5}$'s Object gap (24.2\,pp) exceeds X-VLA's maximum (1.4\,pp) by $17{\times}$.}
87	\label{fig:method_gap}

90	\textbf{Architecture-dependent scoring.} The optimal scoring method depends on the connector (Table~\ref{tab:attention_pruning}). On X-VLA at $k{=}0.5$, attention-based scoring recovers near-baseline performance (${\geq}$92.8\%) while L2 falls $8$--$27$\,pp below random---because the BART encoder's cross-token attention renders L2 norms anti-informative. On $\pi_{0.5}$, attention is catastrophically harmful on Object ($-$30.4\,pp) because attention scores select semantically relevant tokens that lack the spatial precision this architecture depends on. On Long, attention degrades steeply to a plateau ($-$26.5\,pp from $k{=}0.95$ to $k{=}0.85$, then flat through $k{=}0.5$; Figure~\ref{fig:cliff_highlight}).
91	
92	\textbf{Disruption peak.} Random pruning on $\pi_{0.5}$ produces a counterintuitive failure: intermediate removal is \emph{more} destructive than aggressive removal. On Long at $k{=}0.9$, the margin peaks at 46.9\,pp (L2 80.4\% vs.\ Random 33.6\%; Figure~\ref{fig:cliff_highlight}). The margin follows an asymmetric inverted-U: $k{=}0.95$ (25.6\,pp) to crossing zero near $k{\approx}0.71$; at $k{=}0.5$, all methods converge within ${\sim}$2\,pp (Appendix Figure~\ref{fig:margin_profile}). On Object at $k{=}0.85$, random collapses to 3.6--5.6\% while L2 maintains 99.0\% ($+$90\,pp margin). The V-shaped random recovery arises from position aliasing under scattered removal (Section~\ref{sec:analysis}).

0	\section{Experiments}
1	\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
4	\label{sec:exp_setup}
5	
6	We evaluate vision token pruning across six VLA architectures spanning three connector families on LIBERO~\cite{liu2023libero}, which provides four task suites of increasing complexity: Object, Spatial, Goal, and Long (10 tasks $\times$ 50 episodes each).
7	
8	Four models are evaluated in closed-loop control: $\pi_{0.5}$~\cite{black2025pi05} (4B, linear projection, 768 tokens, CV$\,{=}\,$0.055), X-VLA~\cite{zheng2025xvla} (880M, linear + 12-layer BART encoder, 50 tokens, CV$\,{=}\,$0.151), OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024openvla} (7B, 3-layer MLP, 256 tokens, CV$\,{=}\,$0.215), and SmolVLA~\cite{shukor2025smolvla} (450M, pixel-shuffle $4{\times}4$, 128 tokens, CV$\,{=}\,$0.314). Two additional models ($\pi_{0}$~\cite{black2024pi0}, OFT~\cite{kim2025openoft}) are analyzed via offline metrics only. Table~\ref{tab:model_properties} summarizes all six architectures. We prune at $k \in \{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.85, 0.9, 0.95\}$, reporting mean success rate (SR) over 500 episodes.
9	
10	\subsection{Two-Axis Framework Overview}
11	\label{sec:two_axis}
12	
13	\begin{figure}[t]
14	\centering
15	\includegraphics[width=0.75\linewidth]{figures/paper/fig_pruning_overview.pdf}
16	\caption{Pruning degradation on LIBERO-Object (L2 norm scoring). $\pi_{0.5}$ maintains ${\geq}$98\% through $k{=}0.85$, OpenVLA degrades linearly, X-VLA cliffs at $k{=}0.7$, SmolVLA collapses to 0\% at $k{=}0.95$.}
17	\label{fig:pruning_overview}
18	\end{figure}
19	
20	Three qualitatively distinct response patterns emerge across the connector spectrum (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}; Figure~\ref{fig:pruning_overview}); a fourth (OpenVLA, MLP connector) is detailed in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:full_results}.
21	
22	\textbf{$\pi_{0.5}$ (linear, CV$\,{=}\,$0.055).} Low-CV models are \emph{uniformly critical}: which tokens are removed matters more than how many. L2 maintains ${\geq}$98.0\% through $k{=}0.85$, while 
…[truncated 5075 chars]

  "old_string": "\\textbf{Architecture-dependent scoring.} The optimal scoring method depends on the connector (Table~\\ref{tab:attention_pruning}). On X-VLA at $k{=}0.5$, attention-based scoring recovers near-baseline performance (${\\geq}$92.8\\%) while L2 falls $8$--$27$\\,pp below random---because the BART encoder's cross-token attention renders L2 norms anti-informative. On $\\pi_{0.5}$, attention is catastrophically harmful on Object ($-$30.4\\,pp) because attention scores select semantically relevant tokens that lack the spatial precision this architecture depends on. On Long, attention degrades steeply to a plateau ($-$26.5\\,pp from $k{=}0.95$ to $k{=}0.85$, then flat through $k{=}0.5$; Figure~\\ref{fig:cliff_highlight}).",

On Long, attention cliffs $-$26.5\\,pp from $k{=}0.95$ to $k{=}0.85$, then plateaus (Figure~\\ref{fig:cliff_highlight})."

  "old_string": "\\textbf{Disruption peak.} Random pruning on $\\pi_{0.5}$ produces a counterintuitive failure: intermediate removal is \\emph{more} destructive than aggressive removal. On Long at $k{=}0.9$, the margin peaks at 46.9\\,pp (L2 80.4\\% vs.\\ Random 33.6\\%; Figure~\\ref{fig:cliff_highlight}). The margin follows an asymmetric inverted-U: $k{=}0.95$ (25.6\\,pp) to crossing zero near $k{\\approx}0.71$; at $k{=}0.5$, all methods converge within ${\\sim}$2\\,pp (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:margin_profile}). On Object at $k{=}0.85$, random collapses to 3.6--5.6\\% while L2 maintains 99.0\\% ($+$90\\,pp margin). The V-shaped random recovery arises from position aliasing under scattered removal (Section~\\ref{sec:analysis}).",
  "new_string": "\\textbf{Disruption peak.} Random pruning on $\\pi_{0.5}$ is \\emph{more} destructive at intermediate than aggressive ratios. On Long at $k{=}0.9$, the margin peaks at 46.9\\,pp (L2 80.4\\% vs.\\ Random 33.6\\%; Figure~\\ref{fig:cliff_highlight}); at $k{=}0.5$ all methods converge within ${\\sim}$2\\,pp (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:margin_profile}). The V-shaped random recovery arises from position aliasing under scattered removal (Section~\\ref{sec:analysis})."
}

{

  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-2/projects/importance_margin_vla_compress/docs/paper/experiments.tex",
  "old_string": "Vision Token Re-encoder (VTR) addresses the catastrophic cliff through learned re-encoding rather than hard deletion. A cross-attention module (${\\sim}$22M parameters, ${\\sim}$5\\% of SmolVLA) uses $M$ learned queries to attend over all 128 source tokens, recovering SmolVLA from 0.0\\% to over 60\\% SR at 75\\% compression ($M{=}32$: 60.1 $\\pm$ 1.3\\%, 3 seeds). A parameter-matched MLP adapter achieves comparable performance (63.4 $\\pm$ 2.6\\%, $p{=}0.15$), confirming the gain stems from re-encoding itself, not cross-attention specifically. Cross-suite generalization: Spatial 53.2\\%, Goal 56.4\\%, both from 0.0\\%.",
  "new_string": "VTR uses $M$ learned queries cross-attending over all 128 source tokens (${\\sim}$22M parameters, ${\\sim}$5\\% of SmolVLA), recovering from 0.0\\% to over 60\\% SR at 75\\% compression ($M{=}32$: 60.1 $\\pm$ 1.3\\%, 3 seeds; Table~\\ref{tab:vtr_main}). A parameter-matched MLP adapter achieves 63.4 $\\pm$ 2.6\\% ($p{=}0.15$), confirming the gain stems from re-encoding, not cross-attention. Cross-suite: Spatial 53.2\\%, Goal 56.4\\%, both from 0.0\\%."
}
```

{

  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-2/projects/importance_margin_vla_compress/docs/paper/experiments.tex",
  "old_string": "$\\pi_{0.5}$'s random pruning at $k{=}0.9$ collapses to 9.0\\% on Object despite removing only 10\\% of tokens. We isolate the mechanism by comparing hard pruning (physically removes tokens, re-indexes positions) with zero-masking (replaces token embeddings with zeros, preserves positions). Both destroy identical visual information; only hard pruning alters positional structure. Zero-masking preserves 98.0\\% SR ($-$0.2\\,pp from baseline), while hard pruning collapses to 9.0\\%. The 89\\,pp gap is entirely attributable to position disruption: $\\pi_{0.5}$'s linear connector with learned positional encodings tolerates complete signal destruction but not index shifts.",
  "new_string": "$\\pi_{0.5}$'s random pruning at $k{=}0.9$ collapses to 9.0\\% on Object despite removing only 10\\% of tokens. We compare hard pruning (removes tokens, re-indexes positions) with zero-masking (zeros embeddings, preserves positions): zero-masking preserves 98.0\\% SR while hard pruning collapses to 9.0\\%. The 89\\,pp gap is entirely attributable to position disruption---$\\pi_{0.5}$'s linear connector tolerates complete signal destruction but not index shifts."
}
```

  "old_string": "CV predicts where scoring method choice becomes critical (Table~\\ref{tab:model_properties}; Figure~\\ref{fig:method_gap}). Low-CV models are maximally method-sensitive: $\\pi_{0.5}$'s Object gap at $k{=}0.95$ reaches 24.2\\,pp, exceeding X-VLA's maximum (1.4\\,pp) by $17{\\times}$. As CV increases, sensitivity decreases but does not vanish---OpenVLA's method gap at $k{=}0.9$ ranges from $+$7.8\\,pp to $-$4.8\\,pp across suites (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:full_results}). At $k{=}0.5$, method-independence breaks down: $\\Delta$(R$-$L2) on Object is $+$36.0\\,pp ($\\pi_{0.5}$), $+$27.2\\,pp (X-VLA), confirming that random preserves spatial diversity while L2 clusters correlated tokens.",
  "new_string": "CV predicts where scoring method choice becomes critical (Figure~\\ref{fig:method_gap}). $\\pi_{0.5}$'s Object gap at $k{=}0.95$ reaches 24.2\\,pp, exceeding X-VLA's maximum (1.4\\,pp) by $17{\\times}$. At $k{=}0.5$, $\\Delta$(R$-$L2) on Object is $+$36.0\\,pp ($\\pi_{0.5}$), $+$27.2\\,pp (X-VLA), confirming random preserves spatial diversity while L2 clusters correlated tokens."

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-be-idea-gen-0515-2/projects/importance_margin_vla_compress/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
  "old_string": "Importance-based selection is not universally beneficial---it can be actively harmful. At moderate compression, L2 norm selection preserves 98\\% success on $\\pi_{0.5}$ where random yields 5\\% (93\\,pp gap); at aggressive ratios, random outperforms L2 by up to 36\\,pp. Attention-based scoring exhibits within-model reversals across task suites. No scoring method is universally safe: task complexity amplifies connector-dependent patterns, with multi-step tasks magnifying entangling-connector losses by up to $20{\\times}$. A zero-mask ablation isolates the mechanism: on $\\pi_{0.5}$, zeroing 10\\% of tokens preserves 98.0\\% SR while physical removal collapses to 9.0\\%---the 89\\,pp gap is entirely attributable to position disruption, not information loss. For architectures where all deletion strategies fail, a lightweight re-encoding module (${\\sim}$5\\% extra parameters) recovers $>$60\\% success at 75\\% compression.",
  "new_string": "Importance-based selection is not universally beneficial---it can be actively harmful. L2 norm selection preserves 98\\% success on $\\pi_{0.5}$ where random yields 5\\% (93\\,pp gap); at aggressive ratios, random outperforms L2 by up to 36\\,pp. No scoring method is universally safe: task complexity amplifies connector-dependent patterns ($20{\\times}$ from Object to Goal). A zero-mask ablation isolates the mechanism: zeroing 10\\% of tokens preserves 98.0\\% SR while physical removal collapses to 9.0\\%---the 89\\,pp gap is entirely attributable to position disruption, not information loss. For architectures where all deletion fails, a lightweight re-encoding module (${\\sim}$5\\% extra parameters) recovers $>$60\\% success at 75\\% compression."
}
```

</details>

27	      - "Result 3: VTR recovers 60%+ SR where hard pruning yields 0%, M-curve flat (near-lossless at any compression ratio)"

1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
5	\label{sec:exp_setup}
6	
7	We evaluate vision token pruning across six VLA architectures spanning three connector families on LIBERO~\cite{liu2023libero}, which provides four task suites of increasing complexity: Object, Spatial, Goal, and Long (10 tasks $\times$ 50 episodes each).
8	
9	Four models are evaluated in closed-loop control: $\pi_{0.5}$~\cite{black2025pi05} (4B, linear projection, 768 tokens, CV$\,{=}\,$0.055), X-VLA~\cite{zheng2025xvla} (880M, linear + 12-layer BART encoder, 50 tokens, CV$\,{=}\,$0.151), OpenVLA~\cite{kim2024openvla} (7B, 3-layer MLP, 256 tokens, CV$\,{=}\,$0.215), and SmolVLA~\cite{shukor2025smolvla} (450M, pixel-shuffle $4{\times}4$, 128 tokens, CV$\,{=}\,$0.314). Two additional models ($\pi_{0}$~\cite{black2024pi0}, OFT~\cite{kim2025openoft}) are analyzed via offline metrics only. Table~\ref{tab:model_properties} summarizes all six architectures. We prune at $k \in \{0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.85, 0.9, 0.95\}$, reporting mean success rate (SR) over 500 episodes.
10	
11	\subsection{Two-Axis Framework Overview}
12	\label{sec:two_axis}
13	
14	\begin{figure}[t]
15	\centering
16	\includegraphics[width=0.75\linewidth]{figures/paper/fig_pruning_overview.pdf}
17	\caption{Pruning degradation on LIBERO-Object (L2 norm scoring). $\pi_{0.5}$ maintains ${\geq}$98\% through $k{=}0.85$, OpenVLA degrades linearly, X-VLA cliffs at $k{=}0.7$, SmolVLA collapses to 0\% at $k{=}0.95$.}
18	\label{fig:pruning_overview}
19	\end{figure}
20	
21	Three qualitatively distinct response patterns emerge across the connector spectrum (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}; Figure~\ref{fig:pruning_overview}); a fourth (OpenVLA, MLP connector) is detailed in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:full_results}.
22
